{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Use exactly three states when you do alert:**\n  1. **OK** — everything is fine; no DJ action.\n  2. **Warning** — something is off but not broken; say what is wrong and what DJ needs to do.\n  3. **Broken** — something is broken; say what is broken and what DJ needs to do now.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what exact action does DJ need to take?* If the action is for Hermes, say `DJ action: none. Hermes should ...` explicitly.\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n## Report jobs: daily + weekly, with truthful zeroes\n\nSpend-style report jobs are not health alerts. They should be designed as **explicit daily and weekly summaries** with stable cadence and unambiguous windows.\n\nRules:\n- **Daily and weekly should both exist** when the user asks for both. Do not assume a daily job covers the weekly need.\n- **Report the actual ledger result, even if it is zero.** If spend is `0.0000` or the pricing source is `included`, say that plainly instead of implying failure or activity.\n- **Do not infer work happened from the existence of a scheduled run.** A successful cron wakeup is not evidence that any spend events occurred.\n- **Call out pricing caveats explicitly.** If `estimated_cost_usd` is zero because the provider or pricing path is still unresolved, the report should surface that as a caveat, not hide it.\n- **Use the same source of truth for both cadence variants** so the daily and weekly numbers reconcile (same ledger, different window).\n\nFor spend reports, the primary question is: *what did the ledger record in the window?* Not: *did the job run?*\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n## LLM-backed cron jobs: use the shared accounting helper\n\nWhen a cron job calls OpenRouter directly from a script, prefer the shared helper pattern used by Hermes-owned scripts instead of hand-rolling `requests.post(...)` plus ad hoc usage logging.\n\nGuideline:\n- centralize request + spend recording in a shared helper;\n- attach stable metadata like `project_slug`, `source`, `platform`, and a stage name;\n- verify the rollout by compiling the changed scripts and searching for lingering direct OpenRouter URL calls in the active script tree.\n\nThis keeps token tracking, cost estimation, and ledger attribution aligned across future projects.\n\n## Spend reports: use the session insights path, not the raw ledger\n\nFor Hermes daily/weekly spend reporting, the authoritative reporting source is the *session insights* path (`SessionDB` + `InsightsEngine`), not the narrower `agent.spend_ledger` table alone.\n\nWhy:\n- the raw ledger can show only a partial view of spend attribution;\n- `InsightsEngine.generate(days=...)` already exposes `estimated_cost` and `actual_cost` plus session/model/platform breakdowns;\n- daily and weekly reports should answer: estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, and the top models/platforms driving cost.\n\nRecommended reporting shape:\n- *Daily*: last 24h estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, included sessions, unknown pricing sessions, top models, top platforms.\n- *Weekly*: last 7d estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, included sessions, unknown pricing sessions, top models, top platforms.\n\nIf a spend report prints `$0.0000`, do not assume the report is healthy. Check whether the data path is using the session insights engine or a ledger that undercounts/omits the relevant field

[truncated 8354 chars]

{"path":"/opt/data/scripts/feedparser.py","content":"\"\"\"Minimal local feedparser-compatible shim for Hermes podcast scripts.\n\nSupports the subset of the third-party `feedparser` API used by our podcast\ncollection scripts:\n- parse(url_or_text) -> object with `.entries`, `.bozo`, `.bozo_exception`\n- entry fields: title, summary/description, link, id, published/updated,\n  published_parsed/updated_parsed, enclosures, links, itunes_duration\n\nThis is intentionally small and dependency-free so cron jobs keep working even\nwhen the live venv cannot install external packages.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom email.utils import parsedate_to_datetime\nfrom types import SimpleNamespace\nfrom typing import Any, Iterable\nfrom urllib.parse import urlparse\nimport xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\n\nimport requests\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass FeedResult:\n    entries: list[dict[str, Any]]\n    bozo: bool = False\n    bozo_exception: Exception | None = None\n\n\ndef _strip_ns(tag: str) -> str:\n    return tag.split('}', 1)[-1] if '}' in tag else tag\n\n\ndef _text(el: ET.Element | None) -> str:\n    if el is None:\n        return ''\n    return ''.join(el.itertext()).strip()\n\n\ndef _dt_to_struct(dt: datetime | None):\n    if not dt:\n        return None\n    return dt.utctimetuple()\n\n\ndef _parse_dt(value: str | None):\n    if not value:\n        return None\n    try:\n        dt = parsedate_to_datetime(value)\n        if dt.tzinfo is None:\n            dt = dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n        return dt.astimezone(timezone.utc)\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    try:\n        cleaned = value.replace('Z', '+00:00')\n        dt = {"path":"/opt/data/scripts/bs4.py","content":"\"\"\"Minimal BeautifulSoup-compatible shim for Hermes scripts.\n\nImplements just enough for calls like:\n    from bs4 import BeautifulSoup\n    BeautifulSoup(html, 'html.parser').get_text(' ', strip=True)\n\nThis keeps the live cron venv working without the external bs4 package.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport html as _html\n\n\nclass BeautifulSoup:\n    def __init__(self, markup: str, parser: str | None = None):\n        self.markup = str(markup or '')\n\n    def get_text(self, separator: str = '', strip: bool = False) -> str:\n        text = _html.unescape(re.sub(r'<[^>]+>', ' ', self.markup))\n        text = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', text)\n        if strip:\n            text = text.strip()\n        if separator and separator != ' ':\n            text = separator.join(text.split())\n        return text\n"}

### user

```text
Review the conversation above and update two things:

**Memory**: who the user is. Did the user reveal persona, desires, preferences, personal details, or expectations about how you should behave? Save facts about the user and durable preferences with the memory tool.

**Skills**: how to do this class of task. Be ACTIVE — most sessions produce at least one skill update. A pass that does nothing is a missed learning opportunity, not a neutral outcome.

Target shape of the skill library: CLASS-LEVEL skills with a rich SKILL.md and a `references/` directory for session-specific detail. Not a long flat list of narrow one-session-one-skill entries.

Signals that warrant a skill update (any one is enough):
  • User corrected your style, tone, format, legibility, verbosity, or approach. Frustration is a FIRST-CLASS skill signal, not just a memory signal. 'stop doing X', 'don't format like this', 'I hate when you Y' — embed the lesson in the skill that governs that task so the next session starts fixed.
  • Non-trivial technique, fix, workaround, or debugging path emerged.
  • A skill that was loaded or consulted turned out wrong, missing, or outdated — patch it now.

Preference order for skills — pick the earliest that fits:
  1. UPDATE A CURRENTLY-LOADED SKILL. Check what skills were loaded via /skill-name or skill_view in the conversation. If one of them covers the learning, PATCH it first. It was in play; it's the right place.
  2. UPDATE AN EXISTING UMBRELLA (skills_list + skill_view to find the right one). Patch it.
  3. ADD A SUPPORT FILE under an existing umbrella via skill_manage action=write_file. Three kinds: `references/<topic>.md` for session-specific detail OR condensed knowledge banks (quoted research, API docs excerpts, domain notes) written concise and task-focused; `templates/<name>.<ext>` for starter files meant to be copied and modified; `scripts/<name>.<ext>` for statically re-runnable actions (verification, fixture generators, probes). Add a one-line pointer in SKILL.md so future agents find them.
  4. CREATE A NEW CLASS-LEVEL UMBRELLA when nothing exists. Name at the class level — NOT a PR number, error string, codename, library-alone name, or 'fix-X / debug-Y' session artifact. If the name only fits today's task, fall back to (1), (2), or (3).

User-preference embedding: when the user complains about how you handled a task, update the skill that governs that task — memory alone isn't enough. Memory says 'who the user is and what the current situation and state of your operations are'; skills say 'how to do this class of task for this user'. Both should carry user-preference lessons when relevant.

If you notice overlapping existing skills, mention it — the background curator handles consolidation.

Do NOT capture as skills (these become persistent self-imposed constraints that bite you later when the environment changes):
  • Environment-dependent failures: missing binaries, fresh-install errors, post-migration path mismatches, 'command not found', unconfigured credentials, uninstalled packages. The user can fix these — they are not durable rules.
  • Negative claims about tools or features ('browser tools do not work', 'X tool is broken', 'cannot use Y from execute_code'). These harden into refusals the agent cites against itself for months after the actual problem was fixed.
  • Session-specific transient errors that resolved before the conversation ended. If retrying worked, the lesson is the retry pattern, not the original failure.
  • One-off task narratives. A user asking 'summarize today's market' or 'analyze this PR' is not a class of work that warrants a skill.

If a tool failed because of setup state, capture the FIX (install command, config step, env var to set) under an existing setup or troubleshooting skill — never 'this tool does not work' as a standalone constraint.

Act on whichever of the two dimensions has real signal. If genuinely nothing stands out on either, say 'Nothing to save.' and stop — but don't reach for that conclusion as a default.
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "file": "references/spend-reporting.md", "content": "# Hermes spend reporting\n\nThis note captures the fix for daily/weekly spend briefing jobs.\n\n## Root cause\n\nA daily spend briefing originally used `agent.spend_ledger.summarize_spend(...)` over the raw `llm_usage_events` ledger. That path was useful for event-level attribution, but it underreported the full session-level spend picture the user expected.\n\n## Correct reporting source\n\nUse:\n- `hermes_state.SessionDB`\n- `agent.insights.InsightsEngine`\n\nWhy:\n- `InsightsEngine.generate(days=N)` already computes:\n  - `overview.estimated_cost`\n  - `overview.actual_cost`\n  - session counts\n  - token counts\n  - included/unknown pricing session counts\n  - model/platform breakdowns\n- It is the same source used by the built-in `hermes insights` command.\n\n## Recommended daily report fields\n\n- Estimated spend (last 24h)\n- Actual billed spend (last 24h)\n- Sessions (last 24h)\n- Tokens (last 24h)\n- Included sessions\n- Unknown pricing sessions\n- Top models\n- Top platforms\n\n## Recommended weekly report fields\n\n- Estimated spend (last 7d)\n- Actual billed spend (last 7d)\n- Sessions (last 7d)\n- Tokens (last 7d)\n- Included sessions\n- Unknown pricing sessions\n- Top models\n- Top platforms\n\n## Verification\n\nUseful local checks:\n\n``​`bash\npython3 -m hermes_cli.main insights --days 1\npython3 -m hermes_cli.main spend summary --days 1 --by provider\n``​`\n\nThe first command is the canonical report source for daily/weekly spend briefings.\nThe second command is useful for raw ledger comparison, but not as the primary user-facing spend report.\n", "file_type": ".md"}
```

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Use exactly three states when you do alert:**\n  1. **OK** — everything is fine; no DJ action.\n  2. **Warning** — something is off but not broken; say what is wrong and what DJ needs to do.\n  3. **Broken** — something is broken; say what is broken and what DJ needs to do now.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what exact action does DJ need to take?* If the action is for Hermes, say `DJ action: none. Hermes should ...` explicitly.\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n## Report jobs: daily + weekly, with truthful zeroes\n\nSpend-style report jobs are not health alerts. They should be designed as **explicit daily and weekly summaries** with stable cadence and unambiguous windows.\n\nRules:\n- **Daily and weekly should both exist** when the user asks for both. Do not assume a daily job covers the weekly need.\n- **Report the actual ledger result, even if it is zero.** If spend is `0.0000` or the pricing source is `included`, say that plainly instead of implying failure or activity.\n- **Do not infer work happened from the existence of a scheduled run.** A successful cron wakeup is not evidence that any spend events occurred.\n- **Call out pricing caveats explicitly.** If `estimated_cost_usd` is zero because the provider or pricing path is still unresolved, the report should surface that as a caveat, not hide it.\n- **Use the same source of truth for both cadence variants** so the daily and weekly numbers reconcile (same ledger, different window).\n\nFor spend reports, the primary question is: *what did the ledger record in the window?* Not: *did the job run?*\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n## LLM-backed cron jobs: use the shared accounting helper\n\nWhen a cron job calls OpenRouter directly from a script, prefer the shared helper pattern used by Hermes-owned scripts instead of hand-rolling `requests.post(...)` plus ad hoc usage logging.\n\nGuideline:\n- centralize request + spend recording in a shared helper;\n- attach stable metadata like `project_slug`, `source`, `platform`, and a stage name;\n- verify the rollout by compiling the changed scripts and searching for lingering direct OpenRouter URL calls in the active script tree.\n\nThis keeps token tracking, cost estimation, and ledger attribution aligned across future projects.\n\n## Spend reports: use the session insights path, not the raw ledger\n\nFor Hermes daily/weekly spend reporting, the authoritative reporting source is the *session insights* path (`SessionDB` + `InsightsEngine`), not the narrower `agent.spend_ledger` table alone.\n\nWhy:\n- the raw ledger can show only a partial view of spend attribution;\n- `InsightsEngine.generate(days=...)` already exposes `estimated_cost` and `actual_cost` plus session/model/platform breakdowns;\n- daily and weekly reports should answer: estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, and the top models/platforms driving cost.\n\nRecommended reporting shape:\n- *Daily*: last 24h estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, included sessions, unknown pricing sessions, top models, top platforms.\n- *Weekly*: last 7d estimated spend, actual billed spend, sessions, tokens, included sessions, unknown pricing sessions, top models, top platforms.\n\nIf a spend report prints `$0.0000`, do not assume the report is healthy. Check whether the data path is using the session insights engine or a ledger that undercounts/omits the relevant field

[truncated 8354 chars]

{"action":"write_file","name":"cron-job-design","file_path":"references/cron-failure-triage.md","file_content":"# Cron failure triage\n\nUse this when a cron job reports `exit code 1` or delivers a failure alert.\n\n## Triage order\n\n1. **Find the wrapper log** for the exact cron run.\n2. **Read the first traceback / first nonzero failing step.** Do not start with the tail.\n3. **Identify the exact script and interpreter** the cron wrapper used.\n4. **Reproduce the failing step with the same command line** in the live environment.\n5. **Fix the code path first** when the failure is deterministic.\n6. **If the failure is in the runtime path**, make the job robust via a local fallback or shared helper instead of duplicating logic in each script.\n7. **Re-run the same command once** to verify the fix before declaring success.\n\n## Useful checks\n\n- Did the wrapper stop before the digest/report render step?\n- Did the failure come from an import at process start?\n- Is the job using the same interpreter as the cron wrapper?\n- Is there a cleaner shared helper the job should use instead of hand-rolled code?\n\n## Goal\n\nTurn the failing cron into one of:\n- silent when healthy\n- actionable when broken\n- deterministic enough that the same class of failure is less likely to recur\n"}
```
